x = 1, y = 2 ^rPprzgHS

8 possible positions based on
L-Shaped movement a knight can make. ^aiLa7bc1

function minimumKnightMoves(x,y):
    visited = new Set();
    visited.add('0-0');
    queue = [[0,0]]
    
    directions = [[1,2], [2,1], [2,-1],[-1,2],
[-1,-2],[-2,-1],[-2,1],[1,-2]]
    moveCount = 0
    if (x == 0 && y == 0) return 0 
    while queue.length != 0:
        levelSize = queue.length
        moveCount++
        for i = 0 to levelSize - 1:
            [row, col] = queue.shift()
            for [dr, dc] of directions:
                nRow = row+dr
                nCol = col+dc
                if (nRow == x && nCol == y) return moveCount
                key =  `{nRow}-{nCol}`
                if nRow >= -200 && nRow <= 200 && nCol >= -200 && nCol <= 200:
                    if !visited.has(key):
                        queue.push([nRow,nCol])
                        visited.add(key)
    return moveCount ^kXhNPkla

Time Complexity: O(max(|x|, |y|)²) where x and y are the target cell coordinates. Let d = max(|x|, |y|). The minimum number of knight moves to reach (x, y) is proportional to d, since each move covers at most 2 squares in any direction. BFS explores every cell level-by-level up to that depth. After all levels, the visited region extends roughly 2d in each direction, forming a 2D area of side length O(d) containing O(d²) cells. Each cell is processed at most once, giving us O(d²) total work. ^YKchKUb2

Space Complexity: O(max(|x|, |y|)²) where x and y are the target cell coordinates. Let d = max(|x|, |y|). The visited set stores every explored cell. Since BFS explores a 2D region extending O(d) in each direction, the total number of stored cells is O(d²). ^lPFowGgX

class Solution {
    minimumKnightMoves(x: number, y: number): number {
        if (x === 0 && y === 0) return 0;
        const visited = new Set()
        visited.add('0-0')
        const queue = [[0,0]]
        let moveCount = 0
        const directions = [[1,2],[-1,2],[-2,1],[-2,-1],
        [1,-2],[-1,-2],[2,1],[2,-1]]
        while (queue.length !== 0){
            const levelSize = queue.length
            moveCount++
            for (let i = 0; i < levelSize; ++i){
                const [row, col] = queue.shift()
                for (const [dr, dc] of directions){
                    const nRow = row+dr
                    const nCol = col+dc
                    if (nRow === x && nCol === y){
                        return moveCount
                    }
                    const key = `${nRow}-${nCol}`
                    if (nRow >= -200 && nRow <= 200 && nCol >= -200 && nCol <= 200){
                        if (!visited.has(key)){
                            queue.push([nRow, nCol])
                            visited.add(key)
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        return moveCount
    }
} ^huszYd8f
